These propositions indicate the spatial relationships at the crime scene that can be used to identify contradictions.

**Step 3:** Applying these propositions, we see that the testimony suggesting the victim fell on top of the umbrella (Testimony 3) contradicts the evidence showing the umbrella was far from the victim (as implied by Prop 6 and directly shown in Evidence 2). This indicates a spatial contradiction between Phoenix's testimony and the physical evidence at the crime scene.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning used here involves understanding the positions of objects and individuals at the crime scene as described by both testimonies and evidences. By comparing these descriptions, we can identify inconsistencies that suggest a contradiction.

**Step 5:** Given the analysis, the contradiction is between Evidence 2 (which shows the umbrella far from the victim) and Testimony 3 (which states the victim fell on top of his umbrella). This pair highlights a discrepancy in the spatial arrangement of objects at the crime scene.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyzing the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies, we notice a potential discrepancy between the security camera footage (Evidence 10) and Lang's testimonies (Testimony 0-4). The footage shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle arriving on the roof, but there's no footage of the president riding the elevator down. Lang testifies that the president went up to the roof with Judge Gavèlle and that she came back down alone, implying the president might have been pushed off the roof.

**Step 2:** Checking if any spatial propositions apply, we find Prop 1 and Prop 3 relevant. Prop 1 states that if President Wang was on the roof, he was above the floors of the Bigg Building. Prop 3 states that if the elevator was the only way for President Wang to get down, then he was inside the elevator if he was going down.

**Step 3:** These propositions reveal a contradiction because if the president was going down (as implied by Lang's testimony that Judge Gavèlle came back down alone), according to Prop 3, he should have been inside the elevator. However, the security camera footage (Evidence 10) shows no sign of the president getting on the elevator, contradicting the implication of Lang's testimony (Testimony 2 and 3) that the president must have used the elevator or been pushed off the roof.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning here involves understanding the layout and possible movements within the Bigg Building, particularly the access points to and from the roof. The contradiction arises from the combination of the lack of elevator footage and the testimonies suggesting the president's descent.

**Step 5:** Given the analysis, the contradiction lies between Evidence 10 (Security Camera Footage) and Testimony 3 (Lang's statement about no other way for the president to get down without using the elevator).
